# File map: Bambu Studio `libslic3r` and its Rust port

Generated by `python3 scripts/parity/file_map.py`; do not edit by hand. Regenerate it in every round that
touches `crates/libslic3r-rs/src` (`--check` fails when the committed copy is stale). `--json <file>` writes
the same data with every matched function, the ones estimated faithful included.

C++ side: `references/BambuStudio/src/libslic3r` at the pin. Rust side: `crates/libslic3r-rs/src`.

Where the override layer (`libslic3r/bambustudio/libslic3r`) carries a file, that file is read in place of the
engine's, because it is the one `slicer_cli` compiles: `Fill/FillFloatingConcentric.cpp`, `LogSink.cpp`, `Thread.cpp`.

## How to read it

- A **C++ unit** is a `.cpp` together with the header of the same name, or a header on its own.
- Its **Rust file** is the `.rs` whose name has the same words in another casing style
  (`ExPolygonCollection` and `ex_polygon_collection`). Where only the letters agree and the word breaks
  do not (`ExPolygon` and `expolygon`, `GCode` and `gcode`), the pair is listed and flagged *word breaks*.
- Functions are matched by name inside a pair, in any casing style. Constructors, destructors and
  operators have no name to match by and are only counted. Rust test functions are left out.
- **Missing** is a C++ function with no Rust function of that name in the paired file; when a function of
  that name exists in another Rust file, that file is named. **Rust only** is the reverse.
- The **estimate** for a matched function is mechanical. *stub*: the Rust body is `todo!`/`unimplemented!`
  or a few tokens against a large C++ body. *divergent*: under 30% of the C++ body's identifiers and
  numeric constants appear in the Rust body, or the Rust body is under a fifth of its size. *faithful*: at
  least 60% appear, the Rust body is at least half the size, and it carries none of the port's deviation
  markers (`BLOCKED`, `FIDELITY-NOTE`, `TODO: port`, "not ported", "not implemented", `PARTIAL`).
  *partial*: everything between. Bodies under 25 tokens are counted as faithful. The estimate tells where to
  look first; a *faithful* here is not a proof, and a gated function is judged with both arms in its body.
  A body that reaches the same result through other helpers than the C++ names reads as *partial*.
- **Tier** 1 is the way from a model to G-code for an FFF print without supports, which is what the
  Benchy and Majora jobs take; 2 supports and other FFF options; 3 the rest of the FFF library; 4 SLA.
  The tiers are assigned by file-name patterns in the script, not measured from a run.

## Every pair

### `AABBMesh` (.cpp+.hpp) and `aabb_mesh.rs`

Tier 1. C++ functions 20 (plus 5 constructors, 1 destructors, 0 operators), Rust functions 35. Matched 12: faithful 9, partial 3, divergent 0, stub 0.

Not estimated faithful (shared identifiers and constants, Rust size over C++ size):

- partial: `AABBMesh::AABBImpl::squared_distance` :52 and `AABBMesh::squared_distance` :540 (50%, 0.92x)
- partial: `AABBMesh::query_ray_hit` :151 and `AABBMesh::query_ray_hit` :416 (35%, 1.34x)
- partial: `AABBMesh::query_ray_hits` :180 and `AABBMesh::query_ray_hits` :472 (36%, 1.08x)

Missing in the Rust file:

- `AABBMesh::AABBImpl::init` :21 (49 tokens) — in `gcode/avoid_crossing_perimeters.rs`, `gcode/seam_placer.rs`, `libslic3r.rs`
- `AABBMesh::AABBImpl::intersect_ray` :34 (25 tokens) — in `sla/indexed_mesh.rs`
- `AABBMesh::AABBImpl::intersect_ray` :43 (25 tokens) — in `sla/indexed_mesh.rs`
- `AABBMesh::init` :69 (12 tokens) — in `gcode/avoid_crossing_perimeters.rs`, `gcode/seam_placer.rs`, `libslic3r.rs`
- `AABBMesh::load_holes` :105 (6 tokens)
